# PacEdu English Subject Box — Form 6 Printable Worksheets (Days 1–115)

**Status:** ORIGINAL PACEDU DRAFT — PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW — PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION. Not a Ministry-issued or examination-authorized worksheet pack.

**Senior-secondary safeguard:** Confirm the current authorised Form 6 cohort syllabus, prescribed texts, assessment specifications and criteria separately. These original linked-source stimuli are practice material, not prescribed texts.

**Printing:** Each day has four standalone pathway versions. Every page repeats all source extracts and its task. Confirm one selected worksheet per page in Android Chrome.

---

## Day 1 — Term 1, Week 1 — identity, language and society — Oral communication — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 1  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Present and defend a critical interpretation by synthesising evidence from linked sources and responding to a counterargument.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Present a coherent thesis, synthesise at least two source details, qualify the interpretation and respond to a challenge.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE 1 — Interview extract: A community language worker says that a language archive can help younger people hear expressions used by elders. She argues that recordings are valuable when speakers choose what to share and can correct transcripts.

SOURCE 2 — Youth forum comment: A student supports the archive but worries that a recording may be shared beyond its original purpose. The student asks who controls access and whether a contributor can withdraw an item later.

> The committee's draft policy proposes voluntary participation, contributor review and a withdrawal process, but the access rules have not yet been finalised.

**Worked example:** Thesis: an archive can support intergenerational learning only if contributors retain meaningful control. Source 1 supports the educational value; Source 2 identifies risks of reuse. The draft policy responds partly through review and withdrawal, but unfinished access rules remain a limitation.

**Your task:** Prompt: Should the committee proceed with the language archive? Present a critical position using both sources, explain a tension between value and risk, qualify your recommendation and answer a counter-question about cost or access.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and annotated model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read the extracts aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide a comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

**My analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing are accepted. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks may be used. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Synthesis of both sources, clear thesis, analysis of a tension, qualification and reasoned response to a counter-question. Record the learner's actual response, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I compared sources  ☐ I integrated evidence  ☐ I evaluated limitations  ☐ My conclusion is qualified and reasoned

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — record exact authorised Form 6 syllabus title, cohort, edition, prescribed text/assessment criteria and page only after verification.

## Day 2 — Term 1, Week 1 — identity, language and society — Close reading — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 1  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Compare how two writers construct identity and belonging through viewpoint, structure and language.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Develop a comparative interpretation supported by precise evidence from both texts and analysis of the writers' choices.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

TEXT A — The narrator describes a family gathering as a place where familiar expressions return without explanation. The narrator says the words seem to hold the rhythm of earlier conversations, even when some younger relatives answer in another language.

TEXT B — A younger relative describes the same gathering as welcoming but sometimes difficult to enter. The relative understands the stories but does not always know the expressions. When an elder explains a phrase, the younger person feels invited into the conversation rather than tested.

> Both narrators value the gathering, but they notice different experiences within it.

**Worked example:** Both texts present the gathering as meaningful, but Text A emphasises continuity while Text B highlights access and explanation. The contrast shows that belonging can involve shared memory and active invitation.

**Your task:** Compare the writers' perspectives. Identify one similarity and one difference, support each with precise details, and analyse how the difference shapes the reader's understanding of belonging.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and annotated model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read the extracts aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide a comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

**My analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing are accepted. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks may be used. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Comparative thesis, precise evidence from both texts, analysis of viewpoint/structure and a reasoned explanation of difference. Record the learner's actual response, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I compared sources  ☐ I integrated evidence  ☐ I evaluated limitations  ☐ My conclusion is qualified and reasoned

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — record exact authorised Form 6 syllabus title, cohort, edition, prescribed text/assessment criteria and page only after verification.

## Day 3 — Term 1, Week 1 — identity, language and society — Vocabulary and language — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 1  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Evaluate how diction, syntax, register and rhetorical patterns position audiences across linked texts.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Analyse at least three language choices across the sources and explain their cumulative effect on audience and purpose.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

TEXT A — A campaign statement says: 'Our stories are a bridge between generations.' It uses an extended metaphor to present language as connection and continuity.

TEXT B — A policy note states: 'Contributors may submit material subject to review, access controls and withdrawal provisions.' Its formal register foregrounds procedure and accountability.

> A public comment describes the policy as 'cold and complicated'. The phrase expresses frustration but does not identify which procedure causes difficulty.

**Worked example:** The metaphor in Text A appeals to shared identity and emotion. Text B uses formal, technical nouns to communicate rules precisely but may feel distant. The public comment conveys dissatisfaction, although its criticism needs a specific example.

**Your task:** Analyse the metaphor in Text A, the nominalised/formal wording in Text B and the public comment's evaluative phrase. Explain how each positions its audience and suggest one revision that improves clarity without losing purpose.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and annotated model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read the extracts aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide a comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

**My analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing are accepted. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks may be used. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Accurate language analysis across sources, audience positioning and justified revision. Record the learner's actual response, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I compared sources  ☐ I integrated evidence  ☐ I evaluated limitations  ☐ My conclusion is qualified and reasoned

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — record exact authorised Form 6 syllabus title, cohort, edition, prescribed text/assessment criteria and page only after verification.

## Day 5 — Term 1, Week 1 — identity, language and society — Writing and shaping — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 1  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Write a sustained comparative critical essay that synthesises interpretations, evaluates evidence and reaches a nuanced conclusion.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Construct a coherent essay with a comparative thesis, integrated evidence, analysis of authorial choices, evaluation of limitations and nuanced conclusion.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

> A language archive may preserve voices and expressions that are less often heard in public records. One view emphasises the archive's educational value: recordings can support learning between generations and preserve pronunciation, stories and local knowledge. A second view warns that preservation can become extraction if people lose control of how their contributions are stored or reused. A third view argues that consent and access rules can make preservation more ethical, but only when the rules are clear, enforceable and understood by contributors. The central question is therefore not simply whether an archive should exist, but who shapes it, who benefits and who can make decisions about its contents.

**Worked example:** Comparative thesis: preservation and contributor control must be treated as interdependent. Paragraph 1 synthesises the educational case; paragraph 2 analyses the risk of extraction; paragraph 3 evaluates whether consent and access safeguards are sufficient; conclusion gives a qualified judgement.

**Your task:** Write a sustained comparative critical essay responding to: 'An archive preserves culture only when contributors retain control.' Develop a nuanced thesis, compare at least two viewpoints in the passage, analyse language or assumptions, evaluate a limitation and reach a reasoned conclusion.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and annotated model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read the extracts aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide a comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

**My analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing are accepted. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks may be used. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Sustained comparative essay with integrated evidence, analysis of perspectives/choices, evaluation of limitations and nuanced conclusion. Record the learner's actual response, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I compared sources  ☐ I integrated evidence  ☐ I evaluated limitations  ☐ My conclusion is qualified and reasoned

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — record exact authorised Form 6 syllabus title, cohort, edition, prescribed text/assessment criteria and page only after verification.

## Day 6 — Term 1, Week 2 — community narratives and oral histories — Oral communication — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 2  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Present and defend an interpretation of community narratives by comparing testimony and documentary evidence.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Present a clear comparative claim, integrate two details from different source types and explain one limitation.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Oral account: An elder recalls that the old market was a place where families exchanged news, shared food and discussed decisions. The elder says some dates may be uncertain but remembers the relationships clearly.

SOURCE B — Community record: A dated ledger lists stall names, fees and market days for several years. It does not record informal conversations or family gatherings.

> The sources preserve different forms of community experience.

**Worked example:** Comparative claim: the sources show both the market's organised activity and its social role. The ledger provides dated information about stalls; the oral account describes relationships not recorded in the ledger.

**Your task:** Give a structured oral or written response explaining how the sources complement each other. Use one detail from each source, explain one limitation and answer: 'Which source is more useful?' Avoid treating usefulness as the same as total reliability.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and worked model. Compare one source at a time; the teacher may read the text aloud and provide a vocabulary gloss or comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing are accepted. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks may be used. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Comparative claim, evidence from both sources, explanation of different purposes and a qualified judgement about usefulness. Record response mode, evidence selected, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I compared source types  ☐ I used precise evidence  ☐ I evaluated limitations  ☐ My judgement is supported

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify the current authorised Form 6 syllabus, prescribed texts and assessment criteria.

## Day 8 — Term 1, Week 2 — community narratives and oral histories — Vocabulary and language — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 2  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Analyse how diction, metaphor and register shape representations of community narratives.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Explain three language choices and evaluate their combined effect on tone and viewpoint.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete practice sources:**

> The report calls oral histories 'living records'. The phrase suggests that memories remain active when people retell and interpret them. It describes written minutes as 'fixed traces', which highlights their value for checking dates but also suggests that they capture only selected moments. The report warns that neither form should be treated as a complete account. Its measured register encourages readers to compare evidence rather than choose a single source automatically.

**Worked example:** 'Living records' suggests memory changes through retelling; 'fixed traces' suggests documents preserve selected details. The contrast supports a balanced view of source types.

**Your task:** Explain 'living records' and 'fixed traces'. Analyse how the contrast shapes the report's viewpoint. Identify the register and explain why a measured tone suits the purpose.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and worked model. Compare one source at a time; the teacher may read the text aloud and provide a vocabulary gloss or comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing are accepted. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks may be used. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Accurate connotations, analysis of contrast, register identification and explanation of purpose. Record response mode, evidence selected, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I compared source types  ☐ I used precise evidence  ☐ I evaluated limitations  ☐ My judgement is supported

## Day 9 — Term 1, Week 2 — community narratives and oral histories — Critical literacy — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 2  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Evaluate the strengths, limitations and perspectives of oral, visual and written sources.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Compare at least two sources, distinguish corroborated facts from interpretation and explain what evidence is still needed.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Oral interview: A resident recalls that the meeting house was used for community decisions and informal support. The resident cannot recall exact dates.

SOURCE B — Meeting minutes: The records show that formal meetings were held on several dated occasions and list decisions. They do not record informal support.

SOURCE C — Photograph: A photograph shows chairs arranged in a circle inside the meeting house. It is undated and has no caption.

> The sources differ in what they record and how securely they can be dated.

**Worked example:** The minutes provide dated evidence of formal meetings. The interview adds personal interpretation and informal experience. The photograph suggests a gathering arrangement but, without a date or caption, cannot identify the event or prove its purpose.

**Your task:** Evaluate what each source can and cannot establish. Identify one detail that can be corroborated, one interpretation that remains personal testimony, and what further information would help date and contextualise the photograph.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and worked model. Compare one source at a time; the teacher may read the text aloud and provide a vocabulary gloss or comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing are accepted. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks may be used. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Balanced evaluation, distinction between record and interpretation, recognition of photo limits and relevant further evidence. Record response mode, evidence selected, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I compared source types  ☐ I used precise evidence  ☐ I evaluated limitations  ☐ My judgement is supported

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

## Day 10 — Term 1, Week 2 — community narratives and oral histories — Writing and shaping — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 2  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Write a comparative analytical essay that integrates different source types and reaches a reasoned judgement about community memory.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Construct a coherent comparative essay with a thesis, integrated evidence, analysis of source form, evaluation of limitations and a reasoned conclusion.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete practice sources:**

> Community memory may be preserved through oral accounts, written records and photographs. Oral accounts can describe relationships, emotions and informal experiences, but details may change over time. Written records can provide dates and decisions, but they usually record only what an organisation considered important. Photographs preserve visible details from a moment, but without captions or dates their context may be uncertain. A fuller account emerges when sources are compared and their limits are made explicit.

**Worked example:** Thesis: community memory is best understood by comparing sources rather than relying on one form. Oral accounts add lived experience; records add dates and decisions; photographs add visual evidence. A conclusion should state what the combination supports and what remains uncertain.

**Your task:** Write a comparative analytical essay explaining how oral, written and visual sources contribute to community memory. Integrate at least two source types, analyse their different strengths and limits, and reach a reasoned judgement.

**Copper pathway support:** Use the source labels and worked model. Compare one source at a time; the teacher may read the text aloud and provide a vocabulary gloss or comparison frame. Oral, signed, AAC, dictated, Braille or written responses are accepted.

## Day 10 — Term 1, Week 2 — community narratives and oral histories — Writing and shaping — Bronze Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________  
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 2  
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Bronze  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Write a comparative analytical essay that integrates different source types and reaches a reasoned judgement about community memory.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Construct a coherent comparative essay with a thesis, integrated evidence, analysis of source form, evaluation of limitations and a reasoned conclusion.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete practice sources:**

> Community memory may be preserved through oral accounts, written records and photographs. Oral accounts can describe relationships, emotions and informal experiences, but details may change over time. Written records can provide dates and decisions, but they usually record only what an organisation considered important. Photographs preserve visible details from a moment, but without captions or dates their context may be uncertain. A fuller account emerges when sources are compared and their limits are made explicit.

**Worked example:** Thesis: community memory is best understood by comparing sources rather than relying on one form. Oral accounts add lived experience; records add dates and decisions; photographs add visual evidence. A conclusion should state what the combination supports and what remains uncertain.

**Your task:** Write a comparative analytical essay explaining how oral, written and visual sources contribute to community memory. Integrate at least two source types, analyse their different strengths and limits, and reach a reasoned judgement.

**Bronze pathway support:** Use the comparison frame to identify a claim and one detail from each source. Explain one difference before writing your judgement.

## Day 11 — Term 1, Week 3 — place, environment and belonging — Oral communication — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 3
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Defend a comparative interpretation of how different sources represent authority and community decision-making.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Present a coherent comparative thesis, integrate evidence from two sources, qualify an interpretation and respond to a counterargument.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Council minutes extract: The village council record states that a committee was appointed to review the shared water schedule. Three proposals were discussed, and the council agreed to trial a revised timetable for one month. The minutes record the decision and attendance but do not include the full discussion or individual reasons for agreement.

SOURCE B — Resident interview extract: A resident recalls that households with long walking distances asked for earlier collection times. The resident says the trial helped some families but created difficulty for workers who leave home before sunrise. The interview reflects one household's experience and was recorded two weeks after the trial began.

**Worked example:** The sources show different kinds of evidence: the minutes establish the formal decision and trial period, while the interview describes an effect on one household. Neither alone establishes whether the schedule worked for all households.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's central claim or perspective.
2. **Understand:** Explain each source's purpose and one important detail.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked example's comparative method on the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how source form, language or selection shapes interpretation.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence quality, omissions and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent critical comparison for the stated audience.

**Your task:** Give a structured comparative response: Did the trial timetable represent a fair decision? Integrate one detail from each source, explain what each source can and cannot show, qualify your judgement and answer a counter-question about how the council could gather broader evidence.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison frame and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** A qualified comparative thesis with precise evidence from both sources, source limitations and a practical response about broader consultation. Record source details used, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I compared their perspectives ☐ I evaluated limitations ☐ My conclusion follows from evidence

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page, prescribed text expectations and assessment specification.

## Day 12 — Term 1, Week 3 — place, environment and belonging — Close reading — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 3
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Compare how narrative voice, selection and structure construct contrasting interpretations of collective memory.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Analyse contrasting viewpoints and textual choices across two sources, using precise evidence and explaining the limits of each representation.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Oral-history extract: 'When I think of the old landing, I hear the calls before I see the boats. People waited together, shared food and watched for relatives returning from the outer islands. The landing was more than a place to unload cargo; it was where news arrived.'

SOURCE B — Archive catalogue note: 'Landing facility, constructed 1968; repaired 1984; used for passenger and cargo transfer. Records include maintenance schedules, vessel lists and official notices. Personal accounts and informal gatherings are not systematically documented.'

**Worked example:** Source A uses first-person sensory memory and a sequence of sounds and actions to foreground lived experience. Source B uses compressed factual catalogue language and dates to foreground institutional history while acknowledging that personal accounts are absent.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's central claim or perspective.
2. **Understand:** Explain each source's purpose and one important detail.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked example's comparative method on the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how source form, language or selection shapes interpretation.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence quality, omissions and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent critical comparison for the stated audience.

**Your task:** Write a comparative close-reading response. Analyse the effect of voice and structure in both sources, explain how each constructs collective memory, and evaluate what is included or excluded. Do not claim either source is automatically more truthful because it is personal or official.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison frame and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Comparative analysis of voice, register, selection and omission, with balanced evaluation of the evidence each source provides. Record source details used, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I compared their perspectives ☐ I evaluated limitations ☐ My conclusion follows from evidence

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page, prescribed text expectations and assessment specification.

SOURCE A — Oral-history extract: 'When I think of the old landing, I hear the calls before I see the boats. People waited together, shared food and watched for relatives returning from the outer islands. The landing was more than a place to unload cargo; it was where news arrived.'

SOURCE B — Archive catalogue note: 'Landing facility, constructed 1968; repaired 1984; used for passenger and cargo transfer. Records include maintenance schedules, vessel lists and official notices. Personal accounts and informal gatherings are not systematically documented.'

**Worked example:** Source A uses first-person sensory memory and a sequence of sounds and actions to foreground lived experience. Source B uses compressed factual catalogue language and dates to foreground institutional history while acknowledging that personal accounts are absent.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's central claim or perspective.
2. **Understand:** Explain each source's purpose and one important detail.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked example's comparative method on the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how source form, language or selection shapes interpretation.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence quality, omissions and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent critical comparison for the stated audience.

**Your task:** Write a comparative close-reading response. Analyse the effect of voice and structure in both sources, explain how each constructs collective memory, and evaluate what is included or excluded. Do not claim either source is automatically more truthful because it is personal or official.

**Gold pathway support:** Extend through synthesis, nuanced qualification or a counterargument while retaining the same critical objective.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Comparative analysis of voice, register, selection and omission, with balanced evaluation of the evidence each source provides. Record source details used, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I compared their perspectives ☐ I evaluated limitations ☐ My conclusion follows from evidence

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page, prescribed text expectations and assessment specification.

## Day 14 — Term 1, Week 3 — place, environment and belonging — Critical literacy — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 3
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Evaluate how evidence selection, institutional interests and missing perspectives shape competing accounts of development.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Compare source claims, evidence quality, interests and omissions; distinguish established facts from projections and identify further evidence required.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Port authority statement: 'The new passenger terminal is expected to reduce boarding delays and improve visitor services. The project includes a covered waiting area and a revised traffic plan.' The statement provides projected benefits but no before-and-after waiting-time data.

SOURCE B — Transport users' submission: 'Some passengers report that the new route adds walking distance, especially for people carrying goods or using mobility aids. The submission asks for shaded rest points and a review of drop-off access.' It includes three short accounts but no full passenger survey or route measurements.

**Worked example:** The authority statement identifies design features and expected benefits, but the outcome is not yet measured. The submission identifies access concerns from some users, but three accounts cannot establish the experience of all passengers. Route measurements and a broader user study are needed.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's central claim or perspective.
2. **Understand:** Explain each source's purpose and one important detail.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked example's comparative method on the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how source form, language or selection shapes interpretation.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence quality, omissions and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent critical comparison for the stated audience.

**Your task:** Evaluate both sources. Identify their likely purposes, strongest evidence, limitations and missing perspectives. Separate projected outcomes from observed experiences. Recommend four pieces of evidence needed for a fair evaluation of the terminal's effects.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison frame and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Balanced source evaluation that distinguishes projections from measured outcomes, recognises sample limitations and proposes relevant further evidence. Record source details used, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I compared their perspectives ☐ I evaluated limitations ☐ My conclusion follows from evidence

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page, prescribed text expectations and assessment specification.

## Day 15 — Term 1, Week 3 — place, environment and belonging — Writing and shaping — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 3
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Construct a sustained critical essay comparing interpretations of development, access and community experience.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Write a cohesive comparative essay with a defensible thesis, integrated evidence, source evaluation, counterargument and reasoned conclusion.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Community forum summary: Participants welcomed improved visitor facilities but asked that transport changes not make the terminal harder to reach for market vendors, older passengers or people using mobility aids. The summary records recurring concerns but notes that attendance was voluntary and not all user groups were represented.

SOURCE B — Project review note: The terminal plan includes a covered waiting area and revised traffic routes. The review recommends measuring boarding times, walking distances and user satisfaction after opening. It does not yet contain post-opening results.

**Worked example:** A defensible thesis may argue that the project has potential benefits but its success cannot be judged without access and outcome data. Source A records concerns from attendees but is not a complete survey; Source B describes intended features and future measures, not proven outcomes.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's central claim or perspective.
2. **Understand:** Explain each source's purpose and one important detail.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked example's comparative method on the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how source form, language or selection shapes interpretation.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence quality, omissions and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent critical comparison for the stated audience.

**Your task:** Write a sustained critical essay comparing the interpretations in both sources. Establish a thesis, integrate at least two details from each source, evaluate the sources' limitations, address a counterargument and conclude what evidence should determine whether the development has succeeded.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison frame and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; the teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and provide one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** A sustained comparative essay with a clear thesis, integrated evidence, source evaluation, counterargument and reasoned conclusion. Record source details used, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I compared their perspectives ☐ I evaluated limitations ☐ My conclusion follows from evidence

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page, prescribed text expectations and assessment specification.

**Your task:** Write a sustained critical essay comparing the interpretations in both sources. Establish a thesis, integrate at least two details from each source, evaluate the sources' limitations, address a counterargument and conclude what evidence should determine whether the development has succeeded.

**Bronze pathway support:** Use the planning frame and evidence locator. Organise the response as thesis, source evidence, comparison and limitation; reduce prompts gradually.

## Day 15 — Term 1, Week 3 — place, environment and belonging — Writing and shaping — Silver Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 3
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Silver  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Construct a sustained critical essay comparing interpretations of development, access and community experience.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Write a cohesive comparative essay with a defensible thesis, integrated evidence, source evaluation, counterargument and reasoned conclusion.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Community forum summary: Participants welcomed improved visitor facilities but asked that transport changes not make the terminal harder to reach for market vendors, older passengers or people using mobility aids. The summary records recurring concerns but notes that attendance was voluntary and not all user groups were represented.

SOURCE B — Project review note: The terminal plan includes a covered waiting area and revised traffic routes. The review recommends measuring boarding times, walking distances and user satisfaction after opening. It does not yet contain post-opening results.

**Worked example:** A defensible thesis may argue that the project has potential benefits but its success cannot be judged without access and outcome data. Source A records concerns from attendees but is not a complete survey; Source B describes intended features and future measures, not proven outcomes.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's central claim or perspective.
2. **Understand:** Explain each source's purpose and one important detail.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked example's comparative method on the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how source form, language or selection shapes interpretation.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence quality, omissions and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent critical comparison for the stated audience.

**Your task:** Write a sustained critical essay comparing the interpretations in both sources. Establish a thesis, integrate at least two details from each source, evaluate the sources' limitations, address a counterargument and conclude what evidence should determine whether the development has succeeded.

**Silver pathway support:** Work independently. Integrate precise evidence, explain the relationship between sources and qualify the judgement.

**Evidence for teacher:** A sustained comparative essay with a clear thesis, integrated evidence, source evaluation, counterargument and reasoned conclusion. Record source details used, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

## Day 16 — Term 1, Week 4 — power, voice and representation — Oral communication — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 4
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Present a comparative interpretation of how different speakers describe authority and access to public decisions.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Present a coherent comparative claim, integrate two source details and respond to a counter-question.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Committee notice: The district committee will hold one public meeting about the proposed transport route. Written comments may be sent to the office within seven days. The notice gives the meeting time and location but does not mention an online or telephone option.

SOURCE B — Residents' message: Several shift workers say the meeting time overlaps with their work. They support safer transport but ask for another way to comment. The message was prepared by a small residents' group and does not represent every household.

**Worked example:** The notice establishes the formal opportunity to attend or submit comments. The residents' message identifies a possible access barrier for shift workers. Neither source establishes how many people are affected.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's main claim.
2. **Understand:** Explain the perspective in your own words.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked model to compare the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how language, voice or method shapes meaning.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent comparative response.

**Your task:** Explain whether the consultation arrangements appear accessible. Use one detail from each source, qualify your judgement and answer how the committee could broaden participation without delaying the decision unnecessarily.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison grid and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; a teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and give one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Comparative claim, accurate details, recognition of source limits and practical suggestions for accessible participation. Record evidence, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I explained the evidence ☐ I evaluated a limitation ☐ I qualified my conclusion

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page and assessment specification.

## Day 17 — Term 1, Week 4 — power, voice and representation — Close reading — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 4
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Compare how voice, structure and selection shape interpretations of power and representation.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Analyse two source forms and explain how voice, structure and omission shape reader understanding.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Official report: 'The committee completed the consultation phase and received written comments from residents. The preferred route was selected after technical review.'

SOURCE B — Personal account: 'I sent a comment about the steep path near our homes. The final map still shows the same route. I do not know whether my comment was considered because the report does not explain what changed or why.'

**Worked example:** Source A uses concise institutional language and reports completion and selection. Source B uses first-person narrative and a specific example to raise a question about influence. It does not prove the comment was ignored, but it identifies missing feedback.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's main claim.
2. **Understand:** Explain the perspective in your own words.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked model to compare the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how language, voice or method shapes meaning.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent comparative response.

**Your task:** Compare the sources' voice and structure. Analyse the effect of the phrase 'consultation phase', explain what the personal account reveals and identify what evidence would show whether comments influenced the final route.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison grid and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; a teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and give one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Balanced close reading of institutional and personal voice, with distinction between an unanswered question and proof of exclusion. Record evidence, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I explained the evidence ☐ I evaluated a limitation ☐ I qualified my conclusion

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page and assessment specification.

## Day 18 — Term 1, Week 4 — power, voice and representation — Vocabulary and language — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 4
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Analyse how evaluative and procedural language distinguishes consultation activity from meaningful influence.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Explain connotation, register and certainty in linked sources and revise a claim to match available evidence.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Project update: 'The process delivered comprehensive engagement and strong community ownership.'

SOURCE B — Monitoring note: 'Three meetings were held and 46 written comments were logged. The note does not report the number of people invited, the range of groups represented or how comments changed the final design.'

**Worked example:** 'Comprehensive' implies broad coverage; 'ownership' implies meaningful influence. The monitoring note confirms activity counts but does not establish representativeness or influence.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's main claim.

## Day 19 — Term 1, Week 4 — power, voice and representation — Critical literacy — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 4
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Evaluate source methods and evidence when judging whether consultation was representative.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Assess sampling, source perspective, limitations and missing evidence; justify a qualified conclusion.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Online feedback summary: 62% of 150 respondents supported the route. The summary does not state how many people received the link or how respondents were selected.

SOURCE B — Access group note: Members report that some residents could not use the online form because of limited data or inaccessible layout. The note includes five examples but no population-wide count.

**Worked example:** The 62% figure describes respondents, not necessarily all residents. The access note identifies plausible barriers but does not quantify their prevalence. Both sources need further evidence.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's main claim.
2. **Understand:** Explain the perspective in your own words.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked model to compare the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how language, voice or method shapes meaning.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent comparative response.

**Your task:** Evaluate both sources. Explain what the percentage can establish, identify the access group's contribution and limitations, and propose at least three methods for obtaining a more representative view.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison grid and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; a teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and give one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Method-aware judgement distinguishing respondents from population, with justified follow-up methods. Record evidence, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I explained the evidence ☐ I evaluated a limitation ☐ I qualified my conclusion

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page and assessment specification.

## Day 20 — Term 1, Week 4 — power, voice and representation — Writing and shaping — Copper Worksheet

**Learner name:** __________________________________  **School:** ______________________________
**Class/Form:** Form 6  **Subject:** English  **Term/Week:** Term 1 / Week 4
**Date:** __________________  **Ability pathway:** Copper  **Teacher:** ______________________________

**Learning intention:** Write a sustained comparative essay evaluating whether a public decision-making process was transparent and inclusive.

**PacEdu working achievement indicator:** Construct a cohesive essay with thesis, integrated evidence, source evaluation, counterargument and reasoned conclusion.

**Read, listen to or explore these complete linked practice sources:**

SOURCE A — Final committee statement: The committee selected the route after technical review and consultation. It records 150 online responses and three meetings. It says the decision balances safety, cost and access but does not publish a response log showing how comments were considered.

SOURCE B — Community submission: The group supports improved safety but asks for an accessible response log, a clear explanation of route changes and another opportunity for people unable to use the online form. It acknowledges that the committee had to consider cost and engineering limits.

**Worked example:** A defensible thesis may argue that the decision has documented consultation activity but needs a response log to demonstrate how public evidence influenced the outcome. Source A lists activity and criteria; Source B identifies missing accountability while recognising constraints.

**Build-up questions — Bloom's Taxonomy:**
1. **Remember:** Identify each source's main claim.
2. **Understand:** Explain the perspective in your own words.
3. **Apply:** Use the worked model to compare the sources.
4. **Analyse:** Explain how language, voice or method shapes meaning.
5. **Evaluate:** Judge evidence and limitations.
6. **Create:** Present a coherent comparative response.

**Your task:** Write a sustained comparative essay evaluating the transparency and inclusiveness of the decision. Integrate both sources, assess their limits, address the committee's need to consider cost and engineering, and conclude what evidence would demonstrate accountable decision-making.

**Copper pathway support:** Use source labels, a comparison grid and the worked model. Work through one source at a time; a teacher may read aloud, gloss vocabulary and give one prompt at a time.

**My comparative analysis / response:**

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

____________________________________________________________________________

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Sustained comparative argument with thesis, source integration, fair counterargument, limitation analysis and evidence-based conclusion. Record evidence, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I explained the evidence ☐ I evaluated a limitation ☐ I qualified my conclusion

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page and assessment specification.

**Accessible response methods:** Braille, screen reader, large print/high contrast, speech, sign, AAC, pointing, gesture, drawing, eye gaze, dictation or writing. Adapted tools, extra time and sensory breaks are permitted. Do not force speech or handwriting.

**Evidence for teacher:** Sustained comparative argument with thesis, source integration, fair counterargument, limitation analysis and evidence-based conclusion. Record evidence, response mode, accuracy, independence and prompt level.

**Self-check:** ☐ I used both sources ☐ I explained the evidence ☐ I evaluated a limitation ☐ I qualified my conclusion

**Teacher review:** PENDING_TEACHER_REVIEW

**Official syllabus mapping:** PENDING_OFFICIAL_INDICATOR_VERIFICATION — verify exact authorised Form 6 cohort outcome, syllabus title, edition, page and assessment specification.

## Release gate
A qualified Fiji English teacher must review all Form 6 tasks against the current authorized syllabus, cohort, prescribed texts and assessment specifications. Do not release until review, official source mapping and Android Chrome print testing are complete.
